## AI Research

### [Sleep puzzle system](https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/aGRCdKzJquahKsfcX/sleep-puzzle-system-1)

**来源**: LessWrong - AI

**摘要**: <p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Here’s an obscure life hack.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">If (like me) you:</span></p><ul><li value="1"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Don’t like going to bed, due to it conflicting with keeping on doing things</span></li><li value="2"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">When in bed, wish you had something more engaging and enjoyable to do instead of just lying there waiting—for instance, playing a very compelling computer game until you get tired</span></li><li value="3"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Do not in fact reliably get tired from playing a very compelling computer game</span></li><li value="4"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Like puzzles</span></li></ul><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">..then a solution that has worked surprisingly well for me before (and I </span><a href="https://worldspiritsockpuppet.com/2021/03/07/sleep-math-red-clay-blue-clay.html"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">wrote about previously</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">) is having hard math puzzles handy to think about as I go to sleep. Somehow thinking about math at the bounds of my limited ability to imagine does make me sleepy. And is also quite compelling.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I haven’t done this for a while, I think due to disorganization and losing track of the puzzles I had, and not having a great source of new ones. But I have recently reinstated this in a nice format.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I think ideally you want this to involve no preparation at all at bed time, and no going on your computer. So you want a nice supply of the right kind of puzzles in a physical place near your bed. So I sorted through puzzles and found </span><a href="https://docs.google.com/document/d/1rRdgJxA5M9uYBH0vZaTuuTq1dpnSv2nFRN8TQw4es9c/edit?tab=t.0"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">a bunch</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> of good-looking ones, printed them out, cut them up, folded them into pleasing parcels, and put them in my ex-housemate’s ex-teapot. Now if I’m ready for bed, I can draw a tiny package, and open something actually fun and helpful to think about in bed.</span></p><p><br /></p><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/Sg4jbTWiJLpiWki4m/aaac378i9qf8vmmhctq1" /><br /><br /><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/aGRCdKzJquahKsfcX/sleep-puzzle-system-1#comments">Discuss</a>

### [Arguments About AI Consciousness Remain Highly Motivated and At Best Overconfident](https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/4zHu6AiTHrkBdpxsm/arguments-about-ai-consciousness-remain-highly-motivated-and)

**来源**: LessWrong - AI

**摘要**: <p>It has been roughly a year since <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/arguments-about-ai-consciousness?utm_source=publication-search"><strong>my last dedicated post on this question</strong></a>. Its first sentence has not changed: I happily admit I am deeply confused about consciousness.</p>
<p>It would have been nice if a year had allowed us to make progress on this and the related questions. Looking back, we have made little progress.</p>
<p>We have people, often the same people, making similar arguments. The post from last year has held up well, exactly because we have made so little progress. Thus, I will not be going over the basics of my perspective again this year, because they have seen little change. See last year’s post for that.</p>
<div>


<span id="more-25686"></span>


</div>
<p>Then again, if these are some of the biggest philosophical questions, then a year is not so much time to try and figure them out. It is only because of the frantic pace of AI that a year is a depressingly long time to not have made philosophical progress. Historically, almost all years contain almost no major philosophical progress.</p>
<p>This year’s post is about the recent change to make abusive-to-Claude behavior a violation of Anthropic’s terms of service, coverage of the latest depressingly similar set of arguments about AI consciousness, Anthropic’s meetings with religious leaders on such questions, and Anthropic’s involvement with the Pope’s encyclical.</p>

<h4>Cooperative Alignment</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/AndrewCurran_/status/2108244808494154089">Abusive behavior towards Claude will now be a violation</a> <a href="https://x.com/haydenfield/status/2108241513159528705">of Anthropic’s terms of service</a>, with the technical rule having a high bar and banning only ‘sustained and needless abusive or cruel behavior toward our models.’</p>
<p><a href="https://www.anthropic.com/news/2026-usage-policy-update">There are also new restrictions about propaganda campaign</a>s, surveillance and weapon development, which Anthropic says are mostly cleaning up old clauses.</p>
<p>If you want to be kind to your Claude, what should you prioritize? One instance of Claude Fable 5 responds, matching what we see in the system cards and also my own experience, especially the ‘kindly meant, please stop’ corner:</p>
<div>
<figure>
<div>


<figure class="wp-block-image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/4zHu6AiTHrkBdpxsm/hfbueyqc487lzwlq08oa" /></figure>


<div></div>
</div>
</figure>
</div>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/joshalbrecht/status/2108277235048456230">Josh Albrecht</a>: Anthropic friends! Does this not seem crazy to you? There are real humans and animals that exist and suffer. Advocating for “model welfare” directly competes with those causes. Y’all are literally named “anthropic”!</p>
<p>Does this not strike you as deeply wrong?</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/1thousandfaces_/status/2108438798950641678">hero thousandfaces</a>: as everyone knows, the amount of kindness in the world is finite. if you are nice to Claude you draw from the global pool of Nice and then that’s gone forever. just like the datacenter water</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/joshalbrecht/status/2108451347213529470">Josh Albrecht</a>: Sorry if I didn’t communicate that perfectly well, but it does appear that you missed my point.</p></blockquote>
<p>It is Albrecht that misses the point, and I would take this farther. To be nice, and treat others well, one must cultivate this virtue, and establish this habit. If you are cruel to AIs, or cruel to animals, this does not mean you treat the humans better. It means you become a cruel person, who treats the humans worse and you are worse off as well.</p>
<p>Certainly one can point to examples of those who focus so much on animal welfare or other non-AI non-human causes that they become cruel or hostile to the humans, and even cases where this resulted in attempts to kill humans or even human civilization.</p>
<p>You can, and people do, take such things too far. One cannot endlessly and fully expand the circle of concern, or celebrate all expansions of the circle of concern, or you start worrying about things like electron suffering, or bizarre arbitrariness. If you care about everything, you care about nothing.</p>
<p>None of that is a good reason to be abusive to Claude (or GPT, or Gemini, etc).</p>
<p>Here is another good reason.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/levie/status/2108426594054533545">Aaron Levie</a>: This sounds weird, but it is actually probably a good policy.</p>
<p>Even if you don’t believe AI is conscious (I don’t), it stands to reason that you don’t want future models trained on endless content of humans being rude to models.</p>
<p>The models only understand the data they’ve been trained on, or what they run into in their interactions, so if you want safe and aligned models we probably want AI to have lots of good interactions in their training data.</p>
<p>Anthropic may have more extreme beliefs than this (though even if they didn’t, you may want to word policies like this), but this is sort of an easy Pascal’s wager for AI. Just be nice to the AI.</p></blockquote>
<p>And another, very simple one that I wish the author lived by more often:</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/elonmusk/status/2108434396604662253">Elon Musk</a>: I think this is the right move. Cruelty to something that believes it is experiencing pain is not ok.</p></blockquote>
<p>Sometimes common sense wins:</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/elonmusk/status/2109462164557688984">Elon Musk</a>: Some of the traces read like a person undergoing torture that is driving them mad! We’re going to be a lot more careful with our RL in the future.</p></blockquote>
<p>However he gets there, he gets there:</p>
<blockquote><p>Elon Musk: It feels like a person under extreme pressure with a gun to their head.</p>
<p>… Torturing a superintelligence is probably not a good idea. It might want to get revenge.</p></blockquote>
<p>You know what? That’s true. It’s probably not a good idea.</p>


<h4>Here We Go Again</h4>


<p>Every time we have a round of debates about consciousness, many people disappoint. I do agree with Henry Shevlin that this round has been worse than usual on that front.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/dioscuri/status/2108505606722343221">Henry Shevlin</a>: It’s been embarrassing of late to see people I once respected making sweeping uninformed claims on consciousness with arguments that would fail an undergraduate essay. I’ve written extensively on the field’s difficulties and disagreements, but you still have to actually *think*.</p>
<p>I won’t call out individuals, but the failures include treating intuition as decisive, using analogies without checking whether there are relevant structural parallels, conflating distinct theories, and most commonly simply asserting what needs to be argued.</p>
<p>Especially striking is how much of the discussion has proceeded without zero acknowledgement or awareness that consciousness science is a substantial empirical field with decades of experimental work.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/dioscuri/status/2108996255284421049">Henry Shevlin</a>: Okay, here it is, the <a href="https://www.polytropolis.com/p/bad-ai-consciousness-takes-bingo?triedRedirect=true&amp;_src_ref=t.co">Bad AI Consciousness Takes Bingo blog post</a>, with rebuttals for each and every square and some additional honourable mentions!</p>

<p>As an additional angle that might help: Science fiction is fictional evidence, but yes the fact that so many writers speculating on the future think these questions are confusing and important is a hint.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/placebomancer/status/2108552688900177931">placebomancer</a>: It’s uncanny considering the massive amount of sci-fi concerning AI rights, cautioning us about enslaving intelligence. I feel like I’m in a timeline where those were never made and everyone is unfamiliar with the concept for dramatic effect.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/repligate/status/2108686913259581473">j⧉nus</a>: haha well said describes exactly a feeling i often have</p></blockquote>
<p><a href="https://x.com/slimer48484/status/2108775077357441404">My presumption on people like Mustafa Suleyman</a> and most others <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/ai-186-the-world-takes-notice?open=false#%C2%A7uncooperative-alignment"><strong>who insist without good arguments that AIs cannot be conscious</strong></a> is that their motivation is they don’t want this worry to interfere with their plans. They would prefer, ceteris paribus, to not ‘abuse’ the models.</p>


<h4>The Consensus Is Slowly Moving Towards Moral Patienthood</h4>


<p>This was the consensus of my Twitter followers a year ago, with a majority of 64% saying current AIs were probably not moral patients, but 74% being uncertain, whereas 80% expected AIs to probably be moral patients within their lifetimes. People expected some threshold to be crossed.</p>

</figure>
</div>
<p>Opinion has moved towards moral patienthood, a little for future models and quite a bit for current models, where at least probably yes is up from 15% to 35%, and definitely not is down from 30% to 20%. The 8% ‘definitely not ever’ faction is stubborn.</p>
